It can use custom distance metrics for specialized applications.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install scikit-learn numpy\n```\n\n## Basic Usage with Custom Metric\n\n### Method 1: Using Precomputed Distance Matrix\n```python\nfrom sklearn.cluster import DBSCAN\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.spatial.distance import pdist, squareform\n\n# Your points\npoints = np.array([[x1, y1], [x2, y2], ...])\n\n# Define custom distance function\ndef custom_distance(a, b, weight=1.0):\n    dx = a[0] - b[0]\n    dy = a[1] - b[1]\n    return np.sqrt((weight * dx)**2 + ((2 - weight) * dy)**2)\n\n# Compute pairwise distance matrix\nn = len(points)\ndist_matrix = np.zeros((n, n))\nfor i in range(n):\n    for j in range(i+1, n):\n        d = custom_distance(points[i], points[j], weight=1.5)\n        dist_matrix[i, j] = d\n        dist_matrix[j, i] = d\n\n# Run DBSCAN with precomputed distances\ndbscan = DBSCAN(eps=10, min_samples=3, metric='precomputed')\nlabels = dbscan.fit_predict(dist_matrix)\n```\n\n### Method 2: Using Callable Metric\n```python\nfrom sklearn.cluster import DBSCAN\n\n# Define metric as callable\ndef weighted_euclidean(a, b, weight=1.0):\n    dx = a[0] - b[0]\n    dy = a[1] - b[1]\n    return np.sqrt((weight * dx)**2 + ((2 - weight) * dy)**2)\n\n# Use with DBSCAN (must use functools.partial for extra params)\nfrom functools import partial\nmetric_func = partial(weighted_euclidean, weight=1.5)\n\ndbscan = DBSCAN(eps=10, min_samples=3, metric=metric_func)\nlabels = dbscan.fit_predict(points)\n```\n\n## Understanding DBSCAN Parameters\n\n- **eps (epsilon)**: Maximum distance between two points to be considered neighbors\n- **min_samples**: Minimum number of points required to form a dense region (cluster)\n- **metric**: Distance metric to use ('euclidean', 'precomputed', or callable)\n\n## Working with Labels\n\n```python\n# DBSCAN labels: -1 = noise, 0, 1, 2, ... = cluster IDs\nlabels = dbscan.fit_predict(points)\n\n# Get unique clusters (excluding noise)\nclusters = set(labels)\nclusters.discard(-1)  # Remove noise label\n\n# Compute cluster centroids\ncentroids = []\nfor cluster_id in sorted(clusters):\n    cluster_points = points[labels == cluster_id]\n    centroid = cluster_points.mean(axis=0)\n    centroids.append(centroid)\n```\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n- Precomputed distance matrix: O(n²) memory, but allows any custom metric\n- Callable metric: More memory efficient, but may be slower\n- For large datasets, consider approximate nearest neighbor methods\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1333,"cache_read_input_tokens":18094,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1333,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6dc48011-d827-4a70-8ad9-be394b49070a","uuid":"2d51a30d-a892-4bd1-8d86-20db550d7cde"}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01HALbHsvrpcweZ1GMRi3wXP","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01RfByQy8AGBTgHfGS7HTqo1","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/pareto-frontier.md","content":"# Pareto Frontier in Multi-Objective Optimization\n\n## Overview\nA Pareto frontier (or Pareto front) is the set of all Pareto-optimal solutions in multi-objective optimization. A solution is Pareto-optimal if no other solution is better in all objectives simultaneously.\n\n## Concept\n\nFor two objectives where we want to:\n- **Maximize** objective 1 (e.g., F1 score)\n- **Minimize** objective 2 (e.g., error)\n\nA point is Pareto-optimal if there's no other point that:\n- Has higher objective 1 AND lower objective 2\n\n## Implementation in Python\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\ndef find_pareto_frontier(df, maximize_cols=None, minimize_cols=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find Pareto-optimal points from a DataFrame.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - df: DataFrame with objective columns\n    - maximize_cols: List of column names to maximize\n    - minimize_cols: List of column names to minimize\n\n    Returns:\n    - DataFrame containing only Pareto-optimal rows\n    \"\"\"\n    maximize_cols = maximize_cols or []\n    minimize_cols = minimize_cols or []\n\n    # Create a copy for comparison\n    values = df[maximize_cols + minimize_cols].values\n    n = len(values)\n\n    is_pareto = np.ones(n, dtype=bool)\n\n    for i in range(n):\n        if not is_pareto[i]:\n            continue\n\n        # For each point, check if any other point dominates it\n        for j in range(n):\n            if i == j or not is_pareto[j]:\n                continue\n\n            # Check if j dominates i\n            dominates = True\n            strictly_better = False\n\n            # Check maximize objectives (j should be >= i)\n            for k, col in enumerate(maximize_cols):\n                if values[j, k] < values[i, k]:\n                    dominates = False\n                    break\n                if values[j, k] > values[i, k]:\n                    strictly_better = True\n\n            if not dominates:\n                continue\n\n            # Check minimize objectives (j should be <= i)\n            for k, col in enumerate(minimize_cols):\n                idx = len(maximize_cols) + k\n                if values[j, idx] > values[i, idx]:\n                    dominates = False\n                    break\n                if values[j, idx] < values[i, idx]:\n                    strictly_better = True\n\n            # j dominates i if it's at least as good in all objectives\n            # and strictly better in at least one\n            if dominates and strictly_better:\n                is_pareto[i] = False\n                break\n\n    return df[is_pareto].copy()\n\n# Example usage\ndata = {\n    'F1': [0.8, 0.9, 0.7, 0.85],\n    'error': [10, 15, 5, 8],\n    'param1': [1, 2, 3, 4]\n}\ndf = pd.DataFrame(data)\n\n# Find Pareto frontier (maximize F1, minimize error)\npareto_df = find_pareto_frontier(df,\n                                  maximize_cols=['F1'],\n                                  minimize_cols=['error'])\n```\n\n## Visualization\n\n```python\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n# Plot all points and Pareto frontier\nplt.scatter(df['error'], df['F1'], label='All points', alpha=0.5)\nplt.scatter(pareto_df['error'], pareto_df['F1'],\n            label='Pareto frontier', color='red', s=100)\nplt.xlabel('Error (minimize)')\nplt.ylabel('F1 Score (maximize)')\nplt.legend()\nplt.show()\n```\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n1. **Two objectives**: Forms a curve/line in 2D space\n2. **Trade-off**: Moving along the frontier trades one objective for another\n3. **Filtering**: Often filter by minimum thresholds before computing Pareto frontier\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1333,"cache_read_input_tokens":18094,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1333,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6dc48011-d827-4a70-8ad9-be394b49070a","uuid":"cf4c085a-5e93-44ba-a5d7-abc25e50db70"}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01HALbHsvrpcweZ1GMRi3wXP","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01HkPLUMkUUqp14NZucSnEZL","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/greedy-bipartite-matching.md","content":"# Greedy Bipartite Matching\n\n## Overview\nGreedy bipartite matching finds pairs between two sets of points by iteratively selecting the closest unpaired points. While not globally optimal, it's fast and often sufficient for clustering evaluation.\n\n## Use Cases\n- Matching predicted cluster centroids to ground truth labels\n- Associating detections with targets in computer vision\n- Point cloud registration\n\n## Implementation\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.spatial.distance import cdist\n\ndef greedy_match(points_a, points_b, max_distance=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Greedily match points from set A to set B.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - points_a: Array of shape (n, d) - first set of points\n    - points_b: Array of shape (m, d) - second set of points\n    - max_distance: Maximum allowed distance for a match (None = no limit)\n\n    Returns:\n    - matches: List of tuples (idx_a, idx_b, distance)\n    - unmatched_a: Indices from A that weren't matched\n    - unmatched_b: Indices from B that weren't matched\n    \"\"\"\n    if len(points_a) == 0 or len(points_b) == 0:\n        return [], list(range(len(points_a))), list(range(len(points_b)))\n\n    # Compute all pairwise distances\n    distances = cdist(points_a, points_b, metric='euclidean')\n\n    matched_a = set()\n    matched_b = set()\n    matches = []\n\n    # Greedily match closest pairs\n    while True:\n        # Mask out already matched points\n        masked_dist = distances.copy()\n        for i in matched_a:\n            masked_dist[i, :] = np.inf\n        for j in matched_b:\n            masked_dist[:, j] = np.inf\n\n        # Find minimum distance\n        min_dist = masked_dist.min()\n\n        # Stop if no valid matches remain\n        if min_dist == np.inf:\n            break\n        if max_distance is not None and min_dist > max_distance:\n            break\n\n        # Find the pair with minimum distance\n        i, j = np.unravel_index(masked_dist.argmin(), masked_dist.shape)\n\n        matches.append((i, j, min_dist))\n        matched_a.add(i)\n        matched_b.add(j)\n\n    # Find unmatched points\n    unmatched_a = [i for i in range(len(points_a)) if i not in matched_a]\n    unmatched_b = [j for j in range(len(points_b)) if j not in matched_b]\n\n    return matches, unmatched_a, unmatched_b\n\n# Example usage\npredicted = np.array([[10, 20], [30, 40], [50, 60]])\nground_truth = np.array([[12, 22], [48, 58]])\n\nmatches, unmatched_pred, unmatched_gt = greedy_match(\n    predicted, ground_truth, max_distance=10\n)\n\nprint(f\"Matches: {matches}\")\n# Output: [(0, 0, 2.828), (2, 1, 2.828)]\nprint(f\"Unmatched predictions: {unmatched_pred}\")\n# Output: [1]\n```\n\n## Computing F1 Score from Matches\n\n```python\ndef compute_f1_from_matches(n_predicted, n_ground_truth, n_matches):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute F1 score from matching results.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - n_predicted: Number of predicted points/clusters\n    - n_ground_truth: Number of ground truth points\n    - n_matches: Number of successful matches\n\n    Returns:\n    - f1_score: F1 score (0 to 1)\n    \"\"\"\n    if n_predicted == 0 and n_ground_truth == 0:\n        return 1.0  # Perfect if both are empty\n\n    if n_predicted == 0 or n_ground_truth == 0:\n        return 0.0  # No matches possible\n\n    precision = n_matches / n_predicted\n    recall = n_matches / n_ground_truth\n\n    if precision + recall == 0:\n        return 0.0\n\n    f1 = 2 * precision * recall / (precision + recall)\n    return f1\n\n# Example\nf1 = compute_f1_from_matches(\n    n_predicted=3,      # 3 clusters found\n    n_ground_truth=2,   # 2 true clouds\n    n_matches=2         # 2 matched\n)\nprint(f\"F1 Score: {f1:.3f}\")\n# Output: F1 Score: 0.833\n```\n\n## Performance Notes\n\n- Time complexity: O(n*m*min(n,m)) worst case\n- For optimal matching, use Hungarian algorithm (scipy.optimize.linear_sum_assignment)\n- Greedy matching is faster and simpler for most use cases\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1333,"cache_read_input_tokens":18094,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1333,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6dc48011-d827-4a70-8ad9-be394b49070a","uuid":"8cb948c1-2e5e-4175-bffc-8f524e33020b"}

Parallelization can significantly speed up the process when evaluations are independent.\n\n## Options for Parallelization\n\n### 1. Using joblib (Recommended)\n\n```python\nfrom joblib import Parallel, delayed\nimport itertools\n\ndef evaluate_params(param_dict):\n    \"\"\"Evaluate a single parameter combination.\"\"\"\n    # Your evaluation logic here\n    result = some_expensive_function(**param_dict)\n    return {**param_dict, 'score': result}\n\n# Define parameter grid\nparam_grid = {\n    'min_samples': [3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9],\n    'epsilon': range(4, 26, 2),\n    'shape_weight': [round(w, 1) for w in np.arange(0.9, 2.0, 0.1)]\n}\n\n# Generate all combinations\nkeys = param_grid.keys()\nvalues = param_grid.values()\nparam_combinations = [dict(zip(keys, v)) for v in itertools.product(*values)]\n\n# Parallel evaluation\nresults = Parallel(n_jobs=-1, verbose=10)(\n    delayed(evaluate_params)(params) for params in param_combinations\n)\n\n# Convert to DataFrame\nimport pandas as pd\nresults_df = pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n### 2. Using multiprocessing.Pool\n\n```python\nfrom multiprocessing import Pool\nimport itertools\n\ndef evaluate_params(params):\n    \"\"\"Single parameter evaluation.\"\"\"\n    min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight = params\n    # Your evaluation logic\n    score = compute_score(min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight)\n    return {\n        'min_samples': min_samples,\n        'epsilon': epsilon,\n        'shape_weight': shape_weight,\n        'score': score\n    }\n\n# Generate combinations\nmin_samples_vals = [3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9]\nepsilon_vals = list(range(4, 26, 2))\nshape_weight_vals = [round(w, 1) for w in np.arange(0.9, 2.0, 0.1)]\n\nparam_combinations = list(itertools.product(\n    min_samples_vals, epsilon_vals, shape_weight_vals\n))\n\n# Parallel execution\nwith Pool() as pool:\n    results = pool.map(evaluate_params, param_combinations)\n\nresults_df = pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n### 3. Using concurrent.futures\n\n```python\nfrom concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor, as_completed\nimport itertools\n\ndef evaluate_params(min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight):\n    \"\"\"Evaluate single parameter set.\"\"\"\n    # Your logic here\n    return {\n        'min_samples': min_samples,\n        'epsilon': epsilon,\n        'shape_weight': shape_weight,\n        'score': compute_score(min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight)\n    }\n\n# Generate combinations\nparam_combinations = list(itertools.product(\n    [3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9],\n    range(4, 26, 2),\n    [round(w, 1) for w in np.arange(0.9, 2.0, 0.1)]\n))\n\nresults = []\nwith ProcessPoolExecutor() as executor:\n    futures = {\n        executor.submit(evaluate_params, *params): params\n        for params in param_combinations\n    }\n\n    for future in as_completed(futures):\n        results.append(future.result())\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Choose n_jobs wisely**: `-1` uses all CPU cores\n2. **Batch small tasks**: If individual evaluations are fast (<0.1s), batch them\n3. **Memory considerations**: Large datasets may cause memory issues with many workers\n4. **Progress tracking**: Use `verbose` parameter or `tqdm` for progress bars\n\n```python\nfrom tqdm import tqdm\n\n# With tqdm progress bar\nresults = Parallel(n_jobs=-1)(\n    delayed(evaluate_params)(params)\n    for params in tqdm(param_combinations, desc=\"Grid search\")\n)\n```\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Pickling errors**: Ensure all objects can be serialized\n- **Shared state**: Each worker has separate memory; use return values\n- **Random seeds**: Set seeds inside worker functions for reproducibility\n\n```python\ndef evaluate_params(params):\n    import numpy as np\n    np.random.seed(params['seed'])  # Set seed per worker\n    # ... rest of evaluation\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1333,"cache_read_input_tokens":18094,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1333,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6dc48011-d827-4a70-8ad9-be394b49070a","uuid":"829a4746-1a4c-4e9c-9d72-21be6d4a8e7c"}

Essential for per-image processing and aggregation in data analysis.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pandas numpy\n```\n\n## Basic GroupBy Operations\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Sample data\ndf = pd.DataFrame({\n    'image': ['img1', 'img1', 'img2', 'img2', 'img3'],\n    'x': [10, 20, 30, 40, 50],\n    'y': [15, 25, 35, 45, 55]\n})\n\n# Group by image\ngrouped = df.groupby('image')\n\n# Iterate over groups\nfor image_name, group_df in grouped:\n    print(f\"Image: {image_name}\")\n    print(group_df)\n    print()\n```\n\n## Aggregation Functions\n\n```python\n# Single aggregation\nmean_per_image = df.groupby('image')['x'].mean()\n\n# Multiple aggregations\nstats = df.groupby('image').agg({\n    'x': ['mean', 'std', 'count'],\n    'y': ['min', 'max']\n})\n\n# Custom aggregation function\ndef custom_agg(series):\n    return series.max() - series.min()\n\nrange_per_image = df.groupby('image')['x'].agg(custom_agg)\n```\n\n## Apply Custom Functions to Groups\n\n```python\ndef process_group(group_df):\n    \"\"\"Process each group and return a result.\"\"\"\n    # Example: compute centroid\n    centroid_x = group_df['x'].mean()\n    centroid_y = group_df['y'].mean()\n    return pd.Series({\n        'centroid_x': centroid_x,\n        'centroid_y': centroid_y,\n        'n_points': len(group_df)\n    })\n\n# Apply to all groups\nresults = df.groupby('image').apply(process_group)\n```\n\n## Handling Missing Groups\n\n```python\n# Get all unique values from another dataframe\nall_images = pd.Series(['img1', 'img2', 'img3', 'img4'])\n\n# Create results with default values for missing groups\nresults = []\nfor image in all_images:\n    group_df = df[df['image'] == image]\n\n    if len(group_df) == 0:\n        # Handle missing data\n        results.append({\n            'image': image,\n            'value': np.nan,\n            'count': 0\n        })\n    else:\n        # Process normally\n        results.append({\n            'image': image,\n            'value': group_df['x'].mean(),\n            'count': len(group_df)\n        })\n\nresults_df = pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n## Aggregating with NaN Handling\n\n```python\n# Sample results per image\nimage_results = pd.DataFrame({\n    'image': ['img1', 'img2', 'img3', 'img4'],\n    'f1': [0.8, 0.0, 0.9, 0.0],\n    'delta': [5.2, np.nan, 3.1, np.nan]\n})\n\n# Average F1 (include zeros)\navg_f1 = image_results['f1'].mean()\n\n# Average delta (exclude NaN)\navg_delta = image_results['delta'].mean()  # It can use custom distance metrics for specialized applications.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install scikit-learn numpy\n```\n\n## Basic Usage with Custom Metric\n\n### Method 1: Using Precomputed Distance Matrix\n```python\nfrom sklearn.cluster import DBSCAN\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.spatial.distance import pdist, squareform\n\n# Your points\npoints = np.array([[x1, y1], [x2, y2], ...])\n\n# Define custom distance function\ndef custom_distance(a, b, weight=1.0):\n    dx = a[0] - b[0]\n    dy = a[1] - b[1]\n    return np.sqrt((weight * dx)**2 + ((2 - weight) * dy)**2)\n\n# Compute pairwise distance matrix\nn = len(points)\ndist_matrix = np.zeros((n, n))\nfor i in range(n):\n    for j in range(i+1, n):\n        d = custom_distance(points[i], points[j], weight=1.5)\n        dist_matrix[i, j] = d\n        dist_matrix[j, i] = d\n\n# Run DBSCAN with precomputed distances\ndbscan = DBSCAN(eps=10, min_samples=3, metric='precomputed')\nlabels = dbscan.fit_predict(dist_matrix)\n```\n\n### Method 2: Using Callable Metric\n```python\nfrom sklearn.cluster import DBSCAN\n\n# Define metric as callable\ndef weighted_euclidean(a, b, weight=1.0):\n    dx = a[0] - b[0]\n    dy = a[1] - b[1]\n    return np.sqrt((weight * dx)**2 + ((2 - weight) * dy)**2)\n\n# Use with DBSCAN (must use functools.partial for extra params)\nfrom functools import partial\nmetric_func = partial(weighted_euclidean, weight=1.5)\n\ndbscan = DBSCAN(eps=10, min_samples=3, metric=metric_func)\nlabels = dbscan.fit_predict(points)\n```\n\n## Understanding DBSCAN Parameters\n\n- **eps (epsilon)**: Maximum distance between two points to be considered neighbors\n- **min_samples**: Minimum number of points required to form a dense region (cluster)\n- **metric**: Distance metric to use ('euclidean', 'precomputed', or callable)\n\n## Working with Labels\n\n```python\n# DBSCAN labels: -1 = noise, 0, 1, 2, ... = cluster IDs\nlabels = dbscan.fit_predict(points)\n\n# Get unique clusters (excluding noise)\nclusters = set(labels)\nclusters.discard(-1)  # Remove noise label\n\n# Compute cluster centroids\ncentroids = []\nfor cluster_id in sorted(clusters):\n    cluster_points = points[labels == cluster_id]\n    centroid = cluster_points.mean(axis=0)\n    centroids.append(centroid)\n```\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n- Precomputed distance matrix: O(n²) memory, but allows any custom metric\n- Callable metric: More memory efficient, but may be slower\n- For large datasets, consider approximate nearest neighbor methods\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01RfByQy8AGBTgHfGS7HTqo1","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /root/environment/skills/pareto-frontier.md"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6dc48011-d827-4a70-8ad9-be394b49070a","uuid":"31b5f1ff-dc97-4dd1-8a09-716a55f747cd","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/root/environment/skills/pareto-frontier.md","content":"# Pareto Frontier in Multi-Objective Optimization\n\n## Overview\nA Pareto frontier (or Pareto front) is the set of all Pareto-optimal solutions in multi-objective optimization. A solution is Pareto-optimal if no other solution is better in all objectives simultaneously.\n\n## Concept\n\nFor two objectives where we want to:\n- **Maximize** objective 1 (e.g., F1 score)\n- **Minimize** objective 2 (e.g., error)\n\nA point is Pareto-optimal if there's no other point that:\n- Has higher objective 1 AND lower objective 2\n\n## Implementation in Python\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\ndef find_pareto_frontier(df, maximize_cols=None, minimize_cols=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find Pareto-optimal points from a DataFrame.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - df: DataFrame with objective columns\n    - maximize_cols: List of column names to maximize\n    - minimize_cols: List of column names to minimize\n\n    Returns:\n    - DataFrame containing only Pareto-optimal rows\n    \"\"\"\n    maximize_cols = maximize_cols or []\n    minimize_cols = minimize_cols or []\n\n    # Create a copy for comparison\n    values = df[maximize_cols + minimize_cols].values\n    n = len(values)\n\n    is_pareto = np.ones(n, dtype=bool)\n\n    for i in range(n):\n        if not is_pareto[i]:\n            continue\n\n        # For each point, check if any other point dominates it\n        for j in range(n):\n            if i == j or not is_pareto[j]:\n                continue\n\n            # Check if j dominates i\n            dominates = True\n            strictly_better = False\n\n            # Check maximize objectives (j should be >= i)\n            for k, col in enumerate(maximize_cols):\n                if values[j, k] < values[i, k]:\n                    dominates = False\n                    break\n                if values[j, k] > values[i, k]:\n                    strictly_better = True\n\n            if not dominates:\n                continue\n\n            # Check minimize objectives (j should be <= i)\n            for k, col in enumerate(minimize_cols):\n                idx = len(maximize_cols) + k\n                if values[j, idx] > values[i, idx]:\n                    dominates = False\n                    break\n                if values[j, idx] < values[i, idx]:\n                    strictly_better = True\n\n            # j dominates i if it's at least as good in all objectives\n            # and strictly better in at least one\n            if dominates and strictly_better:\n                is_pareto[i] = False\n                break\n\n    return df[is_pareto].copy()\n\n# Example usage\ndata = {\n    'F1': [0.8, 0.9, 0.7, 0.85],\n    'error': [10, 15, 5, 8],\n    'param1': [1, 2, 3, 4]\n}\ndf = pd.DataFrame(data)\n\n# Find Pareto frontier (maximize F1, minimize error)\npareto_df = find_pareto_frontier(df,\n                                  maximize_cols=['F1'],\n                                  minimize_cols=['error'])\n```\n\n## Visualization\n\n```python\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n# Plot all points and Pareto frontier\nplt.scatter(df['error'], df['F1'], label='All points', alpha=0.5)\nplt.scatter(pareto_df['error'], pareto_df['F1'],\n            label='Pareto frontier', color='red', s=100)\nplt.xlabel('Error (minimize)')\nplt.ylabel('F1 Score (maximize)')\nplt.legend()\nplt.show()\n```\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n1. **Two objectives**: Forms a curve/line in 2D space\n2. **Trade-off**: Moving along the frontier trades one objective for another\n3. **Filtering**: Often filter by minimum thresholds before computing Pareto frontier\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01HkPLUMkUUqp14NZucSnEZL","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /root/environment/skills/greedy-bipartite-matching.md"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6dc48011-d827-4a70-8ad9-be394b49070a","uuid":"9ad0d5c7-9c97-4be4-8b60-bc172692b7c9","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/root/environment/skills/greedy-bipartite-matching.md","content":"# Greedy Bipartite Matching\n\n## Overview\nGreedy bipartite matching finds pairs between two sets of points by iteratively selecting the closest unpaired points. While not globally optimal, it's fast and often sufficient for clustering evaluation.\n\n## Use Cases\n- Matching predicted cluster centroids to ground truth labels\n- Associating detections with targets in computer vision\n- Point cloud registration\n\n## Implementation\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.spatial.distance import cdist\n\ndef greedy_match(points_a, points_b, max_distance=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Greedily match points from set A to set B.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - points_a: Array of shape (n, d) - first set of points\n    - points_b: Array of shape (m, d) - second set of points\n    - max_distance: Maximum allowed distance for a match (None = no limit)\n\n    Returns:\n    - matches: List of tuples (idx_a, idx_b, distance)\n    - unmatched_a: Indices from A that weren't matched\n    - unmatched_b: Indices from B that weren't matched\n    \"\"\"\n    if len(points_a) == 0 or len(points_b) == 0:\n        return [], list(range(len(points_a))), list(range(len(points_b)))\n\n    # Compute all pairwise distances\n    distances = cdist(points_a, points_b, metric='euclidean')\n\n    matched_a = set()\n    matched_b = set()\n    matches = []\n\n    # Greedily match closest pairs\n    while True:\n        # Mask out already matched points\n        masked_dist = distances.copy()\n        for i in matched_a:\n            masked_dist[i, :] = np.inf\n        for j in matched_b:\n            masked_dist[:, j] = np.inf\n\n        # Find minimum distance\n        min_dist = masked_dist.min()\n\n        # Stop if no valid matches remain\n        if min_dist == np.inf:\n            break\n        if max_distance is not None and min_dist > max_distance:\n            break\n\n        # Find the pair with minimum distance\n        i, j = np.unravel_index(masked_dist.argmin(), masked_dist.shape)\n\n        matches.append((i, j, min_dist))\n        matched_a.add(i)\n        matched_b.add(j)\n\n    # Find unmatched points\n    unmatched_a = [i for i in range(len(points_a)) if i not in matched_a]\n    unmatched_b = [j for j in range(len(points_b)) if j not in matched_b]\n\n    return matches, unmatched_a, unmatched_b\n\n# Example usage\npredicted = np.array([[10, 20], [30, 40], [50, 60]])\nground_truth = np.array([[12, 22], [48, 58]])\n\nmatches, unmatched_pred, unmatched_gt = greedy_match(\n    predicted, ground_truth, max_distance=10\n)\n\nprint(f\"Matches: {matches}\")\n# Output: [(0, 0, 2.828), (2, 1, 2.828)]\nprint(f\"Unmatched predictions: {unmatched_pred}\")\n# Output: [1]\n```\n\n## Computing F1 Score from Matches\n\n```python\ndef compute_f1_from_matches(n_predicted, n_ground_truth, n_matches):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute F1 score from matching results.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - n_predicted: Number of predicted points/clusters\n    - n_ground_truth: Number of ground truth points\n    - n_matches: Number of successful matches\n\n    Returns:\n    - f1_score: F1 score (0 to 1)\n    \"\"\"\n    if n_predicted == 0 and n_ground_truth == 0:\n        return 1.0  # Perfect if both are empty\n\n    if n_predicted == 0 or n_ground_truth == 0:\n        return 0.0  # No matches possible\n\n    precision = n_matches / n_predicted\n    recall = n_matches / n_ground_truth\n\n    if precision + recall == 0:\n        return 0.0\n\n    f1 = 2 * precision * recall / (precision + recall)\n    return f1\n\n# Example\nf1 = compute_f1_from_matches(\n    n_predicted=3,      # 3 clusters found\n    n_ground_truth=2,   # 2 true clouds\n    n_matches=2         # 2 matched\n)\nprint(f\"F1 Score: {f1:.3f}\")\n# Output: F1 Score: 0.833\n```\n\n## Performance Notes\n\n- Time complexity: O(n*m*min(n,m)) worst case\n- For optimal matching, use Hungarian algorithm (scipy.optimize.linear_sum_assignment)\n- Greedy matching is faster and simpler for most use cases\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Parallelization can significantly speed up the process when evaluations are independent.\n\n## Options for Parallelization\n\n### 1. Using joblib (Recommended)\n\n```python\nfrom joblib import Parallel, delayed\nimport itertools\n\ndef evaluate_params(param_dict):\n    \"\"\"Evaluate a single parameter combination.\"\"\"\n    # Your evaluation logic here\n    result = some_expensive_function(**param_dict)\n    return {**param_dict, 'score': result}\n\n# Define parameter grid\nparam_grid = {\n    'min_samples': [3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9],\n    'epsilon': range(4, 26, 2),\n    'shape_weight': [round(w, 1) for w in np.arange(0.9, 2.0, 0.1)]\n}\n\n# Generate all combinations\nkeys = param_grid.keys()\nvalues = param_grid.values()\nparam_combinations = [dict(zip(keys, v)) for v in itertools.product(*values)]\n\n# Parallel evaluation\nresults = Parallel(n_jobs=-1, verbose=10)(\n    delayed(evaluate_params)(params) for params in param_combinations\n)\n\n# Convert to DataFrame\nimport pandas as pd\nresults_df = pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n### 2. Using multiprocessing.Pool\n\n```python\nfrom multiprocessing import Pool\nimport itertools\n\ndef evaluate_params(params):\n    \"\"\"Single parameter evaluation.\"\"\"\n    min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight = params\n    # Your evaluation logic\n    score = compute_score(min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight)\n    return {\n        'min_samples': min_samples,\n        'epsilon': epsilon,\n        'shape_weight': shape_weight,\n        'score': score\n    }\n\n# Generate combinations\nmin_samples_vals = [3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9]\nepsilon_vals = list(range(4, 26, 2))\nshape_weight_vals = [round(w, 1) for w in np.arange(0.9, 2.0, 0.1)]\n\nparam_combinations = list(itertools.product(\n    min_samples_vals, epsilon_vals, shape_weight_vals\n))\n\n# Parallel execution\nwith Pool() as pool:\n    results = pool.map(evaluate_params, param_combinations)\n\nresults_df = pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n### 3. Using concurrent.futures\n\n```python\nfrom concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor, as_completed\nimport itertools\n\ndef evaluate_params(min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight):\n    \"\"\"Evaluate single parameter set.\"\"\"\n    # Your logic here\n    return {\n        'min_samples': min_samples,\n        'epsilon': epsilon,\n        'shape_weight': shape_weight,\n        'score': compute_score(min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight)\n    }\n\n# Generate combinations\nparam_combinations = list(itertools.product(\n    [3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9],\n    range(4, 26, 2),\n    [round(w, 1) for w in np.arange(0.9, 2.0, 0.1)]\n))\n\nresults = []\nwith ProcessPoolExecutor() as executor:\n    futures = {\n        executor.submit(evaluate_params, *params): params\n        for params in param_combinations\n    }\n\n    for future in as_completed(futures):\n        results.append(future.result())\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Choose n_jobs wisely**: `-1` uses all CPU cores\n2. **Batch small tasks**: If individual evaluations are fast (<0.1s), batch them\n3. **Memory considerations**: Large datasets may cause memory issues with many workers\n4. **Progress tracking**: Use `verbose` parameter or `tqdm` for progress bars\n\n```python\nfrom tqdm import tqdm\n\n# With tqdm progress bar\nresults = Parallel(n_jobs=-1)(\n    delayed(evaluate_params)(params)\n    for params in tqdm(param_combinations, desc=\"Grid search\")\n)\n```\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Pickling errors**: Ensure all objects can be serialized\n- **Shared state**: Each worker has separate memory; use return values\n- **Random seeds**: Set seeds inside worker functions for reproducibility\n\n```python\ndef evaluate_params(params):\n    import numpy as np\n    np.random.seed(params['seed'])  # Set seed per worker\n    # ... rest of evaluation\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

python3\n\"\"\"\nMars Cloud Clustering Optimization\nFinds Pareto frontier of DBSCAN hyperparameters for clustering citizen science annotations.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom sklearn.cluster import DBSCAN\nfrom scipy.spatial.distance import cdist\nfrom joblib import Parallel, delayed\nimport itertools\n\n\ndef custom_distance_matrix(points, shape_weight):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute pairwise distance matrix with custom weighted metric.\n\n    d(a, b) = sqrt((w * Δx)² + ((2 - w) * Δy)²)\n\n    Parameters:\n    - points: Array of shape (n, 2) with [x, y] coordinates\n    - shape_weight: Weight parameter w\n\n    Returns:\n    - Distance matrix of shape (n, n)\n    \"\"\"\n    n = len(points)\n    if n == 0:\n        return np.array([])\n\n    dist_matrix = np.zeros((n, n))\n    for i in range(n):\n        for j in range(i+1, n):\n            dx = points[i, 0] - points[j, 0]\n            dy = points[i, 1] - points[j, 1]\n            d = np.sqrt((shape_weight * dx)**2 + ((2 - shape_weight) * dy)**2)\n            dist_matrix[i, j] = d\n            dist_matrix[j, i] = d\n\n    return dist_matrix\n\n\ndef greedy_match(points_a, points_b, max_distance=100):\n    \"\"\"\n    Greedily match points from set A to set B using standard Euclidean distance.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - points_a: Array of shape (n, 2) - predicted cluster centroids\n    - points_b: Array of shape (m, 2) - expert annotations\n    - max_distance: Maximum allowed distance for a match\n\n    Returns:\n    - matches: List of tuples (idx_a, idx_b, distance)\n    - unmatched_a: Indices from A that weren't matched\n    - unmatched_b: Indices from B that weren't matched\n    \"\"\"\n    if len(points_a) == 0 or len(points_b) == 0:\n        return [], list(range(len(points_a))), list(range(len(points_b)))\n\n    # Compute all pairwise distances using standard Euclidean\n    distances = cdist(points_a, points_b, metric='euclidean')\n\n    matched_a = set()\n    matched_b = set()\n    matches = []\n\n    # Greedily match closest pairs\n    while True:\n        # Mask out already matched points\n        masked_dist = distances.copy()\n        for i in matched_a:\n            masked_dist[i, :] = np.inf\n        for j in matched_b:\n            masked_dist[:, j] = np.inf\n\n        # Find minimum distance\n        min_dist = masked_dist.min()\n\n        # Stop if no valid matches remain\n        if min_dist == np.inf or min_dist > max_distance:\n            break\n\n        # Find the pair with minimum distance\n        i, j = np.unravel_index(masked_dist.argmin(), masked_dist.shape)\n\n        matches.append((i, j, min_dist))\n        matched_a.add(i)\n        matched_b.add(j)\n\n    # Find unmatched points\n    unmatched_a = [i for i in range(len(points_a)) if i not in matched_a]\n    unmatched_b = [j for j in range(len(points_b)) if j not in matched_b]\n\n    return matches, unmatched_a, unmatched_b\n\n\ndef compute_f1_from_matches(n_predicted, n_ground_truth, n_matches):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute F1 score from matching results.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - n_predicted: Number of predicted clusters\n    - n_ground_truth: Number of ground truth points\n    - n_matches: Number of successful matches\n\n    Returns:\n    - f1_score: F1 score (0 to 1)\n    \"\"\"\n    if n_predicted == 0 and n_ground_truth == 0:\n        return 1.0  # Perfect if both are empty\n\n    if n_predicted == 0 or n_ground_truth == 0:\n        return 0.0  # No matches possible\n\n    precision = n_matches / n_predicted\n    recall = n_matches / n_ground_truth\n\n    if precision + recall == 0:\n        return 0.0\n\n    f1 = 2 * precision * recall / (precision + recall)\n    return f1\n\n\ndef evaluate_image(citsci_points, expert_points, min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight):\n    \"\"\"\n    Evaluate DBSCAN clustering for a single image.\n\n    Returns:\n    - f1: F1 score for this image\n    - delta: Average distance for matched centroids (or NaN if no matches)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Handle case where there are no citizen science points\n    if len(citsci_points) == 0:\n        return 0.0, np.nan\n\n    # Handle case where there are no expert points\n    if len(expert_points) == 0:\n        # If citsci found clusters, they're all false positives\n        return 0.0, np.nan\n\n    # Compute custom distance matrix\n    dist_matrix = custom_distance_matrix(citsci_points, shape_weight)\n\n    # Run DBSCAN with precomputed distances\n    dbscan = DBSCAN(eps=epsilon, min_samples=min_samples, metric='precomputed')\n    labels = dbscan.fit_predict(dist_matrix)\n\n    # Get unique clusters (excluding noise label -1)\n    clusters = set(labels)\n    clusters.discard(-1)\n\n    # If no clusters found\n    if len(clusters) == 0:\n        return 0.0, np.nan\n\n    # Compute cluster centroids\n    centroids = []\n    for cluster_id in sorted(clusters):\n        cluster_points = citsci_points[labels == cluster_id]\n        centroid = cluster_points.mean(axis=0)\n        centroids.append(centroid)\n\n    centroids = np.array(centroids)\n\n    # Match centroids to expert annotations using greedy matching\n    matches, _, _ = greedy_match(centroids, expert_points, max_distance=100)\n\n    # Compute F1 score\n    n_predicted = len(centroids)\n    n_ground_truth = len(expert_points)\n    n_matches = len(matches)\n\n    f1 = compute_f1_from_matches(n_predicted, n_ground_truth, n_matches)\n\n    # Compute average delta (average distance for matched pairs)\n    if n_matches > 0:\n        deltas = [dist for _, _, dist in matches]\n        avg_delta = np.mean(deltas)\n    else:\n        avg_delta = np.nan\n\n    return f1, avg_delta\n\n\ndef evaluate_params(min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight, citsci_df, expert_df, all_images):\n    \"\"\"\n    Evaluate a single parameter combination across all images.\n\n    Returns:\n    - Dictionary with parameters and average metrics\n    \"\"\"\n    f1_scores = []\n    deltas = []\n\n    # Loop over all unique images from expert dataset\n    for image_id in all_images:\n        # Get points for this image\n        citsci_image = citsci_df[citsci_df['file_rad'] == image_id]\n        expert_image = expert_df[expert_df['file_rad'] == image_id]\n\n        citsci_points = citsci_image[['x', 'y']].values\n        expert_points = expert_image[['x', 'y']].values\n\n        # Evaluate this image\n        f1, delta = evaluate_image(citsci_points, expert_points,\n                                   min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight)\n\n        f1_scores.append(f1)\n        if not np.isnan(delta):\n            deltas.append(delta)\n\n    # Compute averages\n    avg_f1 = np.mean(f1_scores)  # Include all F1 scores (including 0.0)\n    avg_delta = np.mean(deltas) if len(deltas) > 0 else np.nan  # Exclude NaN\n\n    return {\n        'min_samples': min_samples,\n        'epsilon': epsilon,\n        'shape_weight': shape_weight,\n        'F1': avg_f1,\n        'delta': avg_delta\n    }\n\n\ndef find_pareto_frontier(df, maximize_cols=None, minimize_cols=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find Pareto-optimal points from a DataFrame.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - df: DataFrame with objective columns\n    - maximize_cols: List of column names to maximize\n    - minimize_cols: List of column names to minimize\n\n    Returns:\n    - DataFrame containing only Pareto-optimal rows\n    \"\"\"\n    maximize_cols = maximize_cols or []\n    minimize_cols = minimize_cols or []\n\n    # Create a copy for comparison\n    values = df[maximize_cols + minimize_cols].values\n    n = len(values)\n\n    is_pareto = np.ones(n, dtype=bool)\n\n    for i in range(n):\n        if not is_pareto[i]:\n            continue\n\n        # For each point, check if any other point dominates it\n        for j in range(n):\n            if i == j or not is_pareto[j]:\n                continue\n\n            # Check if j dominates i\n            dominates = True\n            strictly_better = False\n\n            # Check maximize objectives (j should be >= i)\n            for k, col in enumerate(maximize_cols):\n                if values[j, k] < values[i, k]:\n                    dominates = False\n                    break\n                if values[j, k] > values[i, k]:\n                    strictly_better = True\n\n            if not dominates:\n                continue\n\n            # Check minimize objectives (j should be <= i)\n            for k, col in enumerate(minimize_cols):\n                idx = len(maximize_cols) + k\n                if values[j, idx] > values[i, idx]:\n                    dominates = False\n                    break\n                if values[j, idx] < values[i, idx]:\n                    strictly_better = True\n\n            # j dominates i if it's at least as good in all objectives\n            # and strictly better in at least one\n            if dominates and strictly_better:\n                is_pareto[i] = False\n                break\n\n    return df[is_pareto].copy()\n\n\ndef main():\n    print(\"Loading data...\")\n    citsci_df = pd.read_csv('/root/data/citsci_train.csv')\n    expert_df = pd.read_csv('/root/data/expert_train.csv')\n\n    # Get all unique images from expert dataset\n    all_images = expert_df['file_rad'].unique()\n\n    print(f\"Loaded {len(citsci_df)} citizen science annotations\")\n    print(f\"Loaded {len(expert_df)} expert annotations\")\n    print(f\"Total unique images in expert dataset: {len(all_images)}\")\n\n    # Define parameter grid\n    min_samples_vals = [3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9]\n    epsilon_vals = list(range(4, 26, 2))  # 4, 6, 8, ..., 22, 24\n    shape_weight_vals = [round(w, 1) for w in np.arange(0.9, 2.0, 0.1)]\n\n    print(f\"\\nParameter grid:\")\n    print(f\"  min_samples: {min_samples_vals}\")\n    print(f\"  epsilon: {epsilon_vals}\")\n    print(f\"  shape_weight: {shape_weight_vals}\")\n\n    # Generate all combinations\n    param_combinations = list(itertools.product(\n        min_samples_vals, epsilon_vals, shape_weight_vals\n    ))\n\n    total_combinations = len(param_combinations)\n    print(f\"\\nTotal combinations: {total_combinations}\")\n\n    print(\"\\nRunning grid search in parallel...\")\n\n    # Parallel evaluation\n    results = Parallel(n_jobs=-1, verbose=10)(\n        delayed(evaluate_params)(min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight,\n                                citsci_df, expert_df, all_images)\n        for min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight in param_combinations\n    )\n\n    # Convert to DataFrame\n    results_df = pd.DataFrame(results)\n\n    print(f\"\\nTotal results: {len(results_df)}\")\n    print(f\"Results with valid delta: {results_df['delta'].notna().sum()}\")\n\n    # Filter by F1 > 0.5\n    filtered_df = results_df[results_df['F1'] > 0.5].copy()\n    print(f\"Results with F1 > 0.5: {len(filtered_df)}\")\n\n    if len(filtered_df) == 0:\n        print(\"WARNING: No results with F1 > 0.5 found!\")\n        print(\"\\nSample of best F1 scores:\")\n        print(results_df.nlargest(10, 'F1')[['F1', 'delta', 'min_samples', 'epsilon', 'shape_weight']])\n        return\n\n    # Find Pareto frontier (maximize F1, minimize delta)\n    # Note: We need to handle NaN values in delta\n    # Only consider points with valid delta values for Pareto frontier\n    valid_delta_df = filtered_df[filtered_df['delta'].notna()].copy()\n\n    print(f\"Filtered results with valid delta: {len(valid_delta_df)}\")\n\n    if len(valid_delta_df) == 0:\n        print(\"WARNING: No results with both F1 > 0.5 and valid delta!\")\n        return\n\n    pareto_df = find_pareto_frontier(valid_delta_df,\n                                     maximize_cols=['F1'],\n                                     minimize_cols=['delta'])\n\n    print(f\"\\nPareto frontier size: {len(pareto_df)}\")\n\n    # Round values for output\n \"\"\"\n    Compute pairwise distance matrix with custom weighted metric.\n\n    d(a, b) = sqrt((w * Δx)² + ((2 - w) * Δy)²)\n\n    Parameters:\n    - points: Array of shape (n, 2) with [x, y] coordinates\n    - shape_weight: Weight parameter w\n\n    Returns:\n    - Distance matrix of shape (n, n)\n    \"\"\"\n    n = len(points)\n    if n == 0:\n        return np.array([])\n\n    dist_matrix = np.zeros((n, n))\n    for i in range(n):\n        for j in range(i+1, n):\n            dx = points[i, 0] - points[j, 0]\n            dy = points[i, 1] - points[j, 1]\n            d = np.sqrt((shape_weight * dx)**2 + ((2 - shape_weight) * dy)**2)\n            dist_matrix[i, j] = d\n            dist_matrix[j, i] = d\n\n    return dist_matrix\n\n\ndef greedy_match(points_a, points_b, max_distance=100):\n    \"\"\"\n    Greedily match points from set A to set B using standard Euclidean distance.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - points_a: Array of shape (n, 2) - predicted cluster centroids\n    - points_b: Array of shape (m, 2) - expert annotations\n    - max_distance: Maximum allowed distance for a match\n\n    Returns:\n    - matches: List of tuples (idx_a, idx_b, distance)\n    - unmatched_a: Indices from A that weren't matched\n    - unmatched_b: Indices from B that weren't matched\n    \"\"\"\n    if len(points_a) == 0 or len(points_b) == 0:\n        return matched_a = set()\n    matched_b = set()\n    matches = []\n\n    # Greedily match closest pairs\n    while True:\n        # Mask out already matched points\n        masked_dist = distances.copy()\n        for i in matched_a:\n            masked_dist[i, :] = np.inf\n        for j in matched_b:\n            masked_dist[:, j] = np.inf\n\n        # Find minimum distance\n        min_dist = masked_dist.min()\n\n        # Stop if no valid matches remain\n        if min_dist == np.inf or min_dist > max_distance:\n            break\n\n        # Find the pair with minimum distance\n        i, j = np.unravel_index(masked_dist.argmin(), masked_dist.shape)\n\n        matches.append((i, j, min_dist))\n        matched_a.add(i)\n        matched_b.add(j)\n\n    # Find unmatched points\n    unmatched_a = [i for i in range(len(points_a)) if i not in matched_a]\n    unmatched_b = [j for j in range(len(points_b)) if j not in matched_b]\n\n    return matches, unmatched_a, predicted clusters\n    - n_ground_truth: Number of ground truth points\n    - n_matches: Number of successful matches\n\n    Returns:\n    - f1_score: F1 score (0 to 1)\n    \"\"\"\n    if precision = n_matches / n_predicted\n    recall = n_matches / n_ground_truth\n\n    if precision + recall == 0:\n        return 0.0\n\n    f1 = 2 * precision * recall / (precision + recall)\n    return f1\n\n\ndef evaluate_image(citsci_points, expert_points, min_samples, epsilon, shape_weight):\n    \"\"\"\n    Evaluate DBSCAN clustering for a single image.\n\n    Returns:\n    - f1: F1 score maximize_cols=None, minimize_cols=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find Pareto-optimal points from a DataFrame.\n\n    Parameters:\n    - df: DataFrame with objective columns\n    - maximize_cols: List of column names to maximize\n    - minimize_cols: List of column names to minimize\n\n    Returns:\n    - DataFrame containing only Pareto-optimal rows\n    \"\"\"\n    maximize_cols = maximize_cols or dtype=bool)\n\n    for i in range(n):\n        if not is_pareto[i]:\n            continue\n\n        # For each point, check if any other point dominates it\n        for j in range(n):\n            if objectives\n            # and strictly better in at least one\n            if dominates and strictly_better:\n                is_pareto[i] = False\n                break\n\n    return
